# AssetOps production-shaped MVP architecture

**Decision proposal Â· 2026-10-06 Â· Architect Â· branch `task/production-shaped-mvp-architecture`**

## Product first

The [v6.9 interactive mock screens](../../mockups/v69/index.html) ask one question across Portfolio, Site, Performance, Findings, Evidence, Financials, Assets and Work: **what happened, what did it cost or put at risk, what should we do, and what evidence supports that answer?** AssetOps must connect physical operation to cost, revenue, reliability, maintenance burden and asset-life risk without turning missing evidence into false certainty. The Lab demonstrates the source of observations; it is not the source of product conclusions.

The initial usable journey is **Portfolio priority â†’ Site Overview â†’ domain Performance â†’ Finding â†’ financial consequence â†’ Action â†’ later verification**, with an evidence drawer at every claim. A period is explicit and retained through drill-down; each claim retains its own assessed period and revision. Financial cards identify currency, basis, assumptions, uncertainty and overlap. â€œUnknownâ€ includes a reason. Counts, health, and verified value are never inferred from silence or an action being marked complete.

```mermaid
flowchart LR
  W[Physical site / simulated world] --> D[Device, PLC, BMS, meter / simulated device]
  D --> G[Edge gateway / simulated gateway]
  G --> Q[SQLite outbox]
  Q --> H[HTTPS batches of SourceEnvelopes]
  H --> I[Shared ingestion boundary]
  I --> P[(PostgreSQL: accepted evidence and product state)]
  P --> A[Versioned domain analytics]
  A --> F[Finding and consequence revisions]
  F --> R[Read models / APIs]
  R --> U[Shared operator UI]
  P --> R
  L[Private Lab store] -. inspected only in Lab .-> W
```

**Invariant:** replace the simulated gateway with a real gateway that emits equivalent `SourceEnvelope`s and the ingestion, analytics, read models and operator UI remain unchanged. Only explicit source provenance and the Lab entry point differ. At the first real pilot, a contract test must run a real capture or labelled vendor-format fixture through the physical path and compare normalized envelopes and downstream claims with the simulated path. The S2 demo proves the simulated path only.

## Decisions and boundaries

| Decision | Rationale and why now | Deliberately deferred |
| --- | --- | --- |
| One Python/FastAPI modular monolith, React operator UI, Postgres product database. Separate gateway process and gated Lab process. | Current stack already proves the journey; one transactional backend is fast to build and inspect. Separate processes enforce the source boundary. | Microservices, Kubernetes, generic event platform. |
| Versioned canonical envelope at the gateway; central ingestion is the sole acceptance authority. | Physical and simulated sources need exactly one public contract, with per-record refusal and provenance. | Edge Findings, edge financial analysis, edge portfolio decisions. |
| Direct authenticated HTTPS batch upload with durable edge SQLite outbox. | A pilot can tolerate intermittent connectivity without an always-on subscription or site broker. Batches are replayable and auditable. | MQTT broker, Kafka, dedicated stream processor. |
| Postgres for time-valid configuration, accepted evidence, claim revisions, work and read projections; object storage for large sealed artifacts. | Joins, transactions, revision history and multi-site reads are product requirements now. Artifact bytes need integrity and cheap retention, not relational querying. | Time-series database, data lake, distributed cache. |
| Small domain seam: `site_type` + versioned semantic role bindings + one analytic/read-model module per domain. | Mini-grid, cold-chain and EV share workflow but need different proof and language. | Plugin discovery, user-authored analytic DSL, universal asset ontology. |

### Edge gateway

Adapters poll or subscribe to Modbus/serial, meters, PLC/BMS and vendor HTTP/OPC-UA interfaces as actually encountered at a pilot; each yields a typed device report with source clock, unit, quality and device identity. Site-local, versioned mapping binds `(device, signal)` to a canonical signal/semantic role, applies documented unit conversion, and rejects unknown or incompatible readings. The gateway generates `SourceEnvelope` after mapping, with `source_time`, `published_at`, quality/unavailable reason, interval bounds where relevant, gateway/source/device/signal IDs, mapping version and a stable message identity. Backend sets `received_at`; the gateway cannot impersonate it. Clock skew is recorded/flagged, not silently corrected. An explicit site timezone is presentation context; instants are UTC.

SQLite in WAL mode contains gateway identity/configuration, mapping snapshots, local monotonic sequence allocation per stable source stream, append-only envelope outbox, upload batches/acknowledgements and compact health counters. Write envelope and sequence in one transaction before upload. Preserve bytes and IDs across restarts, backoff with jitter, retry identical batch bytes, and delete/compact only after the server confirms each envelope's accepted/duplicate/rejected/conflict outcome and the acknowledgement is durable locally. Rejected/conflicting messages move to an inspectable dead-letter state; a whole-batch refusal retains the batch for operator repair. Ordering is by source time plus sequence for analysis, never by arrival order; late records trigger recomputation of affected periods. A sequence gap is a source-health signal, not fabricated telemetry. Expose last device contact, queue age/depth, disk pressure, clock offset, last successful upload and mapping errors locally and centrally. Limit disk use with alerts and explicit retention policy; do not silently drop unacknowledged evidence.

**Contract move required:** current `SourceEnvelope` derives `message_id` from site/gateway/source/device/signal/time and has `sequence` outside that identity (`contracts/assetops_contracts/source_envelope.py`). Two legitimate samples at the same timestamp can collide. Introduce an additive new envelope version whose identity includes stable source event ID or persisted sequence; retain parsers for historic v1/v2 records and reject identity reuse with different content. Define sequence scope, reset/epoch and batch acknowledgement schema before a real pilot. Never regenerate IDs on retry. Gateway â€œintelligenceâ€ stops at acquisition, mapping, validation, buffering and diagnostics; cross-device inference, Findings, economic models and verification stay central.

### Ingestion and transport

Use `POST /api/ingestion/batches` over TLS with gateway credentials scoped to site/source, bounded batch size, rate limits and a queryable batch receipt. Authenticate the declared site and gateway against registered identity; do not trust `source_mode` or mapping/version references merely because they are present in JSON. Resolve the mapping and Foundation version effective **at source time**, including old versions after edits. Validate schema, clocks, units, role binding and provenance, then atomically persist acceptance decision, raw envelope digest/reference, normalized accepted record or rejection/conflict, and batch progress in Postgres. Unique keys for `(site, gateway, source, message_id)` and `(site, batch_id)` make retries idempotent; same key and different bytes is a conflict, not overwrite. Return per-envelope disposition and a receipt only after commit. Do not let a malformed sibling hide valid records. An outbox/work table in the same transaction schedules analytic recomputation; a worker leases jobs, retries safely and publishes revisioned read models. The UI exposes ingestion lag so â€œno finding yetâ€ is not interpreted as healthy.

Direct HTTPS is sufficient while pilots have tens of sites, bounded upload volume, and workers can keep up with Postgres. Upgrade to MQTT when device/site topology or near-real-time command/control requires brokered sessions; add a managed queue between receipt and workers when sustained ingest spikes or independent consumer fan-out exceed Postgres job capacity; adopt streaming infrastructure only when measured throughput/latency/replay demands it. These change transport or processing, not the envelope or acceptance semantics.

### Central product model

Keep explicit relational entities: `Tenant/Site`, `Asset` (stable physical identity), `FoundationRevision` (valid-time, recorded-time, topology, objectives, controls), `SignalBindingRevision`, `Gateway/Source`, `IngestionBatch`, `AcceptedEvidence`, `EvidenceRejection`, `AnalyticRun/AssessmentRevision`, `Finding` + immutable `FindingRevision`, `FinancialAssumptionRevision`, `FinancialConsequenceRevision`, `Action/ActionEvent`, `VerificationPlanRevision/Outcome`. Evidence retains raw digest, original and normalized value/unit, timestamps, quality, mapping/configuration revision, and provenance. No retroactive rewrite of a past basis: backdated corrections create a new revision and re-evaluation with links to both versions.

Analytics are pure, versioned functions of accepted evidence, time-valid public configuration and declared assumption versions. Each output records evidence IDs/window, model version, claim level, gaps/alternatives and input digest. A Finding has stable identity and separate lifecycle events; changed evidence creates a revision, not a new indistinguishable row. A consequence names technical driver, quantity, formula, currency, assumption revision, measured/modelled/extrapolated/scenario basis and overlap group. Never add incompatible currencies, windows or overlapping causes. Verification compares a later accepted window with the plan's target **and** service/asset guardrails; return Verified, Ineffective or Inconclusive, with later recurrence able to reopen the Finding.

Use Postgres partitions/indexes on `(site_id, source_time)` if real evidence volume warrants them; ordinary indexed tables are enough initially. Read projections can be Postgres tables/materialized views or bounded query services. Cache only reproducible, keyed views by site/window/domain/revision and invalidate on late evidence, changed configuration or analytic version. The database is the source of truth; a cache is never a second authority.

### Simulator and real-site equivalence

| Boundary | Real site | Simulated site |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Operation | Equipment and environment | World/kernel computes causal operation and private truth |
| Reporting | Device/PLC/BMS/meter samples, gaps, bias, events | Simulated devices sample the world with cadence, gaps, bias and faults |
| Gateway | Protocol adapters, mappings, SQLite outbox, HTTPS | Same gateway contract and SQLite outbox; virtual device adapter and faultable link |
| Crossing | `SourceEnvelope` batch | Same `SourceEnvelope` batch, `SIMULATED` provenance |
| Product | Shared ingestion â†’ Postgres â†’ analytics â†’ APIs â†’ UI | Exactly the same |

The world owns physics, controls, scenario causes and oracle expectations; simulated devices own only what their real counterparts could report. The simulated gateway cannot read private world state except through device reports. Its SQLite store persists staged envelopes and upload state exactly as a physical gateway would. Preserve Lab Draft/Stage/Commit only as a **release gate**: Draft may execute and inspect private truth; Stage seals public envelopes; Commit authorizes delivery through the same batch API and records a manifest/receipt. Commit cannot write evidence, Findings or site projections. Private truth, controller intent, causes, oracle and run controls live in a separate Lab store and Lab-only API with access controls; no foreign key or import from normal analytics to those objects. Simulated provenance may label a claim, never alter its calculation.

### Domain seam and screen reads

The shared shell owns identity, site/window selection, evidence drawer, claim status, Findings, money and work navigation. A small registry keyed by `site_type` selects explicit evidence roles, analytic functions, summary sections and labels. A role binding maps physical signals to analytic inputs for a *valid period*; it is not a runtime plugin. Missing roles lower or block claim capability. Mini-grid roles include generator state/power, feeder demand, PV availability, battery reserve/SOC, fuel and service baseline; cold-chain roles include chamber temperature, setpoint/policy, door/defrost, compressor and backup state; EV roles include session request/energy/end state, connector availability, charger limits and tariff. Keep roles minimal per analytic, not one global schema that pretends domains are interchangeable.

| Screen / API family (window and revision are explicit) | Read model and source | Domain difference |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Portfolio `GET /portfolio` | Site assessment coverage, ranked Finding index, consequence rollup, work/verification summaries | Priorities and consequence categories retain domain and currency; no opaque score. |
| Site Overview `GET /sites/{id}/overview` | Site objective cards, bounded metrics, timeline, latest Finding/consequence/action, source freshness | Mini-grid service/dispatch; cold temperature/backup; EV sessions/availability. |
| Performance `GET /sites/{id}/performance/{view}` | Windowed analytic revisions plus evidence coverage | Dispatch/service/fuel; temperature/cooling/backup; sessions/availability/energy. |
| Findings `GET /findings`, `/findings/{id}` | Stable Finding + revisions, alternatives, consequence and work links | Domain-specific mechanism, severity and claim limits. |
| Evidence `GET /sites/{id}/evidence`, `/evidence/{id}` | Capability matrix, accepted/raw/normalized records, source health, lineage, rejections | Role coverage required for each domain claim. |
| Financials `GET /sites/{id}/financials`, `/financials` | Consequence revisions, assumption versions and compatible rollups | Fuel/maintenance, product/reliability exposure, EV revenue/service loss; show unpriced risk. |
| Assets `GET /assets`, `/assets/{id}` | Asset identity, condition/stress evidence, warranty and linked work | Battery life, compressor/backup health, charger/connector condition. |
| Actions `GET /actions`, `/actions/{id}` | Plan, events, later-window guardrails, verification outcome | Plan target and guardrails follow Finding's domain. |

These are API families, not a command to build every mock screen immediately. Site-scoped lists filter the **same** global Finding/Action objects. Routes without a supported read model stay out of navigation; supported routes with missing evidence show a reason and next acquisition step. Example claim path: generator state/power and PV/battery/load envelopes â†’ accepted records tied to mapping/Foundation revisions â†’ versioned dispatch interval classification â†’ Finding revision + bounded fuel/maintenance consequence under assumption revision â†’ Site/Portfolio projection â†’ Finding card and evidence drawer. The drawer resolves exact IDs and original envelope bytes; it never reconstructs lineage from a card label.

### Storage ownership

| Store | Holds | Reason and boundary |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Edge SQLite | Device mapping snapshot, sequence state, envelope outbox, batch receipts, local diagnostics | Durable offline acquisition; not the central evidence authority. |
| Central Postgres | Site/asset/configuration/assumptions, acceptance ledger, normalized evidence, analytic and Finding revisions, work and read projections | Transactional multi-site source of product truth. |
| Immutable object/artifact storage | Sealed raw batch/artifact bytes, large exports, optional Lab run artifacts; content hash and retention metadata in Postgres | Cheap integrity-preserving blobs; do not query them for UI rows. |
| Private Lab storage | Frozen scenarios, world trajectories, oracle, controller intent, Draft/Stage/Commit manifests | Gated simulator inspection only; never a product analytic input. |
| Derived cache/projection | Site cards, indexes, window aggregates | Rebuildable from Postgres evidence/configuration and model versions. |

## Current code disposition

Classification is about production direction, not a promise to preserve current APIs byte for byte.

| Disposition | Major pieces and rationale |
| --- | --- |
| **KEEP** | `contracts/assetops_contracts/source_envelope.py` strict parsing and provenance shape; `simulator/assetops_simulator/kernel/` causal world and `observation/` separation; `frontend/src/ui/` primitives, operator/Lab shell split, evidence inspection and useful Site/dispatch presentation. They protect the real/simulated boundary or already express the intended journey. |
| **ADAPT** | `simulator/.../gateway/` mapping/publication into a durable SQLite gateway with retry; `contracts/.../ingestion_batch.py` and `backend/.../evidence/service.py` into authenticated, transactional Postgres acceptance and per-record receipts; `backend/.../analytics/dispatch.py` into role-bound, windowed domain analytics; Site Foundation into time-valid configuration; `frontend/src/sites/` into domain-shaped read views. Preserve behavior through equivalence tests, not storage formats. |
| **REPLACE** | JSON whole-site evidence (`backend/.../evidence/adapters/json_evidence_store.py`), JSON assessment store, YAML mutable Site store and process-local write lock with Postgres repositories. Whole-file rewrites and local locks cannot provide concurrent multi-site product semantics. Keep Lab run inputs/artifacts private, moving large immutable run bytes to artifact storage as needed. Replace direct Lab in-process Commit-to-ingest call with the same authenticated batch boundary used by real gateways. |
| **RETIRE** | Hard-coded demonstration device keys in `backend/.../analytics/dispatch.py`; view calculations that independently reinterpret evidence; treating `source_mode` as sufficient authentication; old T0xx sequence as the implementation plan. These encode a single fixture or a demo deployment assumption. Keep historical fixtures/tests as migration evidence, not product authority. |

Present state observed: Commit, accepted Evidence, Site Overview and one mini-grid dispatch Finding exist; persistence is mainly JSON/YAML files, `SITE_TYPES` excludes EV, dispatch inputs name exact demo devices, and many v6.9 mock destinations remain unimplemented. The earlier [v6.9 pivot recommendation](assetops-v69-pivot-recommendation.md) is useful screen context, but this architecture resets storage, gateway and delivery order around real-site substitution.

## Fresh vertical-slice sequence

1. **One evidence path, two sources.** Version/fixture the envelope and batch receipt; build SQLite gateway outbox and authenticated HTTPS/Postgres ingestion. S2 proves the path with simulated input only. The first real pilot supplies one real capture or labelled vendor-format wire fixture to prove the physical adapter input and equivalent downstream behavior. Demo: unplug/reconnect, retries, deduplication, late arrival, rejection and inspectable Evidence lineage.
2. **Mini-grid decision with money.** Migrate Site/Foundation revisions and dispatch analytic to role-bound accepted evidence; produce windowed Finding and bounded fuel/maintenance consequence with editable, versioned assumptions. Demo: Portfolio â†’ Site â†’ dispatch Finding â†’ raw evidence and exact money basis.
3. **Action and verification.** Persist recommendation/Action, plan revision, activity and later-window outcome with service/reserve guardrails. Demo: change a policy in Lab, commit later evidence, show Verified/Ineffective/Inconclusive and supported portfolio change.
4. **Cold-chain equivalence.** Add temperature/backup device roles and a bounded excursion/backup analytic, relevant asset and consequence view. Use the same gateway/API/shell/workflow. Demo: cold room source loss versus real excursion, with honest product-exposure wording.
5. **EV equivalence and estate view.** Add session/connector roles, interruption/availability analytic, revenue assumption and EV asset view; finish comparable Portfolio prioritization across three domains. Demo: one mini-grid, one cold-chain and one EV site, each traceable from source to action.

Each slice ends with a screen-visible claim and an evidence-lineage check. Real-device drivers may start with one pilot protocol; broader protocol libraries, MQTT, stream infrastructure, predictive maintenance, extensive financial scenarios and generic domain plugins wait for observed need. At the first real pilot, the acceptance gate is a real or labelled vendor-format gateway batch replacing the simulator batch while backend, analytics and UI tests remain unchanged. S2â€“S7 demo acceptance uses simulated input and makes no physical protocol proof claim.
# AssetOps MVP technical design

**Architect proposal Â· 2026-10-06.** Implements [the system architecture](production-shaped-mvp.md). This is a target contract for the first vertical slices, not a description of tables already deployed. Examples use short aliases (`t1`, `s1`, `g1`) for UUID values solely to keep the tables readable; real IDs are UUIDs. All money is decimal, never binary float. Times are UTC `timestamptz`; presentation uses the Site timezone.

## 1. Central PostgreSQL model

### Conventions and migration rules

- Use UUID primary keys generated by the writer, `created_at` on identity rows, and explicit tenant scope. Every Site-owned row carries `tenant_id` and `site_id`; composite foreign keys to `(tenant_id, site_id)` prevent cross-tenant references. `site` has `UNIQUE(tenant_id, id)`. Child tables additionally validate a referenced row belongs to the same site in one write transaction; add composite uniqueness/FKs where a row can be reached directly by API ID.
- Natural identities (`site_code`, `gateway_external_id`, `source_external_id`, `device_id`, `signal_id`) are site-scoped. A gateway is never silently reassigned to another site. `source_mode` is immutable registered provenance (`LIVE` or `SIMULATED`), not caller-provided authority.
- For public configuration use valid time `[valid_from, valid_to)` and recorded time `[recorded_at, superseded_at)`. A correction inserts new rows and closes only the replaced rows' recorded-time intervals; their content and old validity ranges are never edited. At a requested **source instant and knowledge instant**, select a row whose both intervals contain those instants. Publishing a new effective version that splits an open interval inserts a new row for the unchanged earlier segment (same content, new revision ID/version) and a row for the changed later segment, then supersedes the old open row. Enforce no overlapping *currently recorded* valid ranges for the same site/configuration key with a GiST exclusion constraint (`btree_gist`), or with a transactionally locked validation query until the extension is enabled. A backdated correction schedules recomputation; existing evidence retains the version used on original acceptance and can be reinterpreted only in a new derived revision.
- Immutable rows are append-only: database privileges and repository methods allow `INSERT`, not content `UPDATE`/`DELETE`; `superseded_at`, lifecycle pointers and job leases are the few named mutable columns. Status derived from event history is a projection. Use `jsonb` only for versioned domain payloads, not identity, time, joins, amount or evidence lineage. Add `schema_version` where payloads are JSON.
- Application writes operate at `READ COMMITTED` with row locks and unique constraints for identity/idempotence. Time-valid configuration publication uses a serializable transaction or locks the Site row. Use DB migrations; never infer schema from YAML files. Scope queries by tenant at the repository boundary and test tenant isolation before external pilots.

### Identity, configuration and acquisition

Each row below gives purpose; key columns and example; relations/constraints/indexes; and mutation/time policy. `FK site` means `(tenant_id, site_id) â†’ site(tenant_id,id)`.

| Table | Purpose; PK; columns; example row | FKs, unique constraints and indexes | Mutation / time |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `tenant` | Estate owner; `id`; `slug`, `name`, `default_currency`, `created_at`; `t1 / acme / KES` | `UNIQUE(slug)`; index `slug` | Name/currency editable with audit; identity immutable. Currency is a default, never a conversion rule. |
| `site` | Place and domain; `id`; `tenant_id`, `site_code`, `name`, `site_type`, `timezone`, `lifecycle`; `s1 / t1 / KOBO / MINIGRID / Africa/Nairobi` | FK `tenant`; `UNIQUE(tenant_id,site_code)`, `UNIQUE(tenant_id,id)`; index `(tenant_id,site_type,lifecycle)` | Identity/type fixed after evidence exists; name/timezone changes audited. Type enum initially `MINIGRID,COLDCHAIN,EV_CHARGING`. |
| `asset` | Physical component with its own work/life history; `id`; `tenant_id`, `site_id`, `asset_code`, `asset_type`, `parent_asset_id`, `commissioned_at`, `retired_at`, `attributes jsonb`; `a1 / s1 / BAT-01 / BATTERY` | FK site, self FK parent constrained to site; `UNIQUE(site_id,asset_code)`; indexes `(site_id,asset_type)`, `(site_id,retired_at)` | Stable ID; registration metadata changes via audited events/revisions when claims depend on it. Retirement is an event/time, not deletion. |
| `gateway` | Credentialed edge identity; `id`; `tenant_id`, `site_id`, `gateway_external_id`, `mode`, `credential_ref`, `registered_at`, `disabled_at`; `g1 / s1 / edge-kobo-01 / SIMULATED` | FK site; `UNIQUE(tenant_id,gateway_external_id)`, `UNIQUE(tenant_id,site_id,id)`; index `(site_id,disabled_at)` | Site and mode immutable; credential reference rotates; never persist secret material here. |
| `source` | Stable stream owner below gateway; `id`; `tenant_id`, `site_id`, `gateway_id`, `source_external_id`, `device_kind`, `registered_at`, `disabled_at`; `src1 / g1 / genset-plc` | FK site and composite FK gateway/site; `UNIQUE(gateway_id,source_external_id)`; index `(site_id,gateway_id)` | Gateway affiliation fixed; replace physical source with new identity if needed. |
| `foundation_revision` | Objectives, topology, controls and physical ratings effective at a time; `id`; `tenant_id`, `site_id`, `version`, `valid_from`, `valid_to`, `recorded_at`, `superseded_at`, `content jsonb`, `content_sha256`; `f1 / s1 / v3 / [10:00,âˆž) / reserve=30%` | FK site; `UNIQUE(site_id,version)`, `UNIQUE(site_id,id)`; GiST current-valid exclusion per site; index `(site_id,valid_from DESC)` | Content/hash/valid range immutable. Close recorded interval on correction; publish replacement rows with new versions, including any unchanged segment. |
| `signal_binding_revision` | Versioned map from device channel to canonical signal and analytic role; `id`; `tenant_id`, `site_id`, `gateway_id`, `source_id`, `binding_set_id`, `version`, `device_id`, `signal_id`, `asset_id`, `role`, `source_unit`, `canonical_unit`, `conversion jsonb`, `valid_from/to`, `recorded_at/superseded_at`; `b1 / src1 / generator-controller.status / generator_state / enum` | FK site, gateway, source, optional asset all same site; `UNIQUE(site_id,binding_set_id,version,device_id,signal_id)`; GiST no current channel overlap; indexes `(site_id,role,valid_from)`, `(source_id,device_id,signal_id,valid_from)` | Binding row immutable except recorded closure. One set/version may contain many rows. Role vocabulary is code-owned per domain/analytic; mapping publication is atomic for its whole set. |

`foundation_revision` holds the public Site definition, not simulated world inputs. `signal_binding_revision` is both the server's registered gateway mapping and the semantic role binding; gateway downloads an immutable versioned snapshot with a canonical content hash over an authenticated channel. A mapping set must reference an effective Foundation revision and every bound asset/channel must exist at publication. The raw envelope still states its mapping set/version for audit. A batch is homogeneous in mapping and Foundation reference; the gateway closes one batch and opens another when either changes. Ingestion checks that the **referenced registered versions' original valid ranges** cover each envelope's source time, even if a later configuration correction superseded them while the gateway was offline. It records the accepted-as basis and flags an outdated mapping/configuration; it rejects unregistered versions or times outside the referenced range. Analytics at a later `knowledge_at` may rebind and renormalize the immutable original reading under a corrected time-valid version, recording those new revision IDs in its input set. This never edits accepted evidence. A record dated before any configured period is rejected with a specific reason; it is not assigned current configuration.

### Ingestion and evidence ledger

| Table | Purpose; PK; columns; example row | FKs, unique constraints and indexes | Mutation / time |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `ingestion_batch` | One immutable upload identity and durable receipt; `id`; `tenant_id`, `site_id`, `gateway_id`, `source_id`, `external_batch_id`, `payload_sha256`, `schema_version`, `foundation_revision_id`, `binding_set_id/version`, `status`, `total/processed/accepted/duplicate/rejected/conflict_count`, `received_at`, `completed_at`; `ib1 / s1 / batch-007 / sha256:â€¦ / COMPLETED / 12` | FK site/gateway/source/foundation same site; `UNIQUE(site_id,external_batch_id)`, `UNIQUE(tenant_id,site_id,id)`; index `(site_id,received_at DESC)` | Payload digest, ordering and reference immutable; counters/status advance transactionally. Same ID/digest returns prior receipt; same ID/different digest yields batch conflict, without changing prior receipt. |
| `accepted_evidence` | One accepted public source fact; `id`; `tenant_id`, `site_id`, `batch_id`, `gateway_id`, `source_id`, `message_id`, `sequence_epoch`, `sequence`, `device_id`, `signal_id`, `asset_id`, `family`, `source_time`, `published_at`, `received_at`, `interval_start/end`, `quality`, `original_value_text/unit`, `normalized_value_text/unit`, `typed_value jsonb`, `envelope_sha256`, `envelope_text`, `foundation_revision_id`, `binding_revision_id`, `source_mode`; `e1 / s1 / msg:â€¦ / 10:02 / generator_state=RUNNING` | FK site/batch/gateway/source/foundation/binding same site; `UNIQUE(site_id,gateway_id,source_id,message_id)`; indexes `(site_id,source_time DESC)`, `(site_id,device_id,signal_id,source_time)`, `(site_id,binding_revision_id,source_time)`, `(batch_id)` | Append-only; never update a normalized value or replace a conflicting envelope. Retain canonical text initially in Postgres; move large raw bytes to immutable object storage later with hash and locator. |
| `evidence_rejection` | Inspectable per-envelope rejection or ID conflict; `id`; `tenant_id`, `site_id`, `batch_id`, `ordinal`, `message_id?`, `kind`, `field`, `detail`, `envelope_sha256`, `conflicts_with_evidence_id?`, `received_at`; `r1 / ib1 / 4 / UNIT_INCOMPATIBLE / e1?` | FK site/batch, optional accepted evidence same site; `UNIQUE(batch_id,ordinal,kind,envelope_sha256)`; indexes `(site_id,received_at DESC)`, `(batch_id,ordinal)` | Append-only. Duplicate retry of a rejected entry returns same disposition, not a new rejection. Whole-batch refusals use `ordinal=NULL` and a distinct `(site_id,external_batch_id,digest)` identity in a batch-refusal ledger if no batch was accepted. |
| `ingestion_disposition` | Exact per-ordinal receipt for retries, including duplicates; PK `(batch_id,ordinal)`; `message_id?`, `decision`, `accepted_evidence_id?`, `rejection_id?`, `decided_at`; `ib1 / 3 / DUPLICATE / e1` | FK batch, optional evidence/rejection same site; index `(batch_id,decision)` | Append-only; in same transaction as evidence decision and batch watermark. Decisions: `ACCEPTED,DUPLICATE,REJECTED,CONFLICT`. |
| `recompute_job` | Transactional work queue; `id`; `tenant_id`, `site_id`, `domain`, `window_start/end`, `cause_evidence_id?`, `status`, `available_at`, `lease_until`, `attempts`; `j1 / s1 / dispatch / [10:00,11:00) / PENDING` | FK site/evidence; index `(status,available_at)` with pending filter; index `(site_id,window_start)` | Lease/status mutable. Coalesce overlapping dirty windows under site/domain lock; never lose the accepted record if worker fails. |

For a complete batch refusal before parsing, store a `batch_refusal(id,tenant_id,site_id?,external_batch_id,payload_sha256,kind,detail,received_at)` row only when tenant/site are authenticated and resolvable; otherwise return a structured error without a Site ledger write. Its unique key is `(tenant_id,external_batch_id,payload_sha256)`. Authentication failures are security logs, not evidence. This keeps malformed batch attempts visible without pretending they were accepted batches.

`gateway_diagnostic` is the central operational-health table, separate from `accepted_evidence`: `id`, `tenant_id`, `site_id`, `gateway_id`, `sequence_epoch`, `diagnostic_sequence`, `observed_at`, `received_at`, `last_device_contact_by_source jsonb`, `queue_count`, `oldest_queue_age_seconds?`, `disk_pressure`, `clock_offset_ms?`, `mapping_error_count`, `payload_sha256`. Use a same-Site gateway FK, `UNIQUE(gateway_id,sequence_epoch,diagnostic_sequence)` and indexes `(site_id,received_at DESC)` and `(gateway_id,received_at DESC)`. Same identity/hash is an idempotent retry; changed bytes with the same identity are refused and audited. Rows are append-only. The server, not the gateway clock, sets `received_at` and computes current contact freshness from a declared heartbeat cadence. A stale/missing report never becomes a healthy-device assertion. This table and `GatewayDiagnosticsV1` are the recorded `A-edge-diagnostics` contract; S2-01c/S2-02 refine cadence and limits.

### Assessments, claims and work

| Table | Purpose; PK; columns; example row | FKs, unique constraints and indexes | Mutation / time |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `analytic_run` | Reproducible computation over one input set; `id`; `tenant_id`, `site_id`, `analytic_id`, `model_version`, `window_start/end`, `knowledge_cutoff`, `input_sha256`, `status`, `started_at`, `completed_at`, `error_code?`; `ar1 / s1 / dispatch / v3 / [10:00,11:00) / hash:â€¦` | FK site; `UNIQUE(site_id,analytic_id,model_version,window_start,window_end,input_sha256)`; index `(site_id,analytic_id,window_start DESC)` | Input identity fixed; execution status mutable. Same key is idempotent. Store exact evidence ID set in `analytic_run_evidence(run_id,evidence_id)` PK pair, both same site. |
| `assessment_revision` | Immutable analytic conclusion, including Unknown/Indeterminate; `id`; `tenant_id`, `site_id`, `analytic_run_id`, `assessment_key`, `revision_no`, `window_start/end`, `claim_level`, `coverage jsonb`, `result jsonb`, `basis_sha256`, `created_at`; `as1 / ar1 / genset-interval-10:05 / rev2 / CANDIDATE_AVOIDABLE` | FK site/run; `UNIQUE(site_id,analytic_id,assessment_key,revision_no)` (store `analytic_id` here); index `(site_id,analytic_id,window_start DESC)` | Append-only. Stable `assessment_key` from analytic identity + physical interval/asset anchor, not from run ID. Latest is a query/projection. |
| `finding` | Stable issue identity and workflow owner; `id`; `tenant_id`, `site_id`, `finding_key`, `domain`, `asset_id?`, `opened_at`, `closed_at?`; `fi1 / s1 / dispatch:genset-10:05 / mini_grid` | FK site/optional asset; `UNIQUE(site_id,finding_key)`; indexes `(site_id,closed_at)`, `(tenant_id,opened_at DESC)` | Identity immutable; close/reopen through events and projection, not by replacing revisions. |
| `finding_revision` | Exact visible claim at one assessment basis; `id`; `tenant_id`, `site_id`, `finding_id`, `assessment_revision_id`, `revision_no`, `title`, `severity`, `claim_level`, `mechanism`, `window_start/end`, `limitations jsonb`, `created_at`; `fr1 / fi1 / as1 / rev2 / Generator support merits review` | FK site/finding/assessment; `UNIQUE(finding_id,revision_no)`, `UNIQUE(finding_id,assessment_revision_id)`; index `(site_id,created_at DESC)` | Append-only; no update when a model or late evidence changes the conclusion. |
| `financial_assumption_revision` | Time-valid price/valuation basis; `id`; `tenant_id`, `site_id?`, `assumption_key`, `revision_no`, `currency`, `unit`, `value numeric(24,8)`, `basis`, `valid_from/to`, `recorded_at/superseded_at`, `author_id`; `fa1 / s1 / diesel_price / v2 / KES / 180 per L` | FK tenant/site if scoped; `UNIQUE(site_id,assumption_key,revision_no)` with separate tenant-scope rule when `site_id IS NULL`; GiST current-valid exclusion per scope/key; index `(site_id,assumption_key,valid_from DESC)` | Value immutable; recorded closure only. Explicitly distinguish observed tariff, contract rate, and scenario assumption. |
| `financial_consequence_revision` | Bounded impact linked to a claim; `id`; `tenant_id`, `site_id`, `finding_revision_id`, `assessment_revision_id`, `assumption_revision_id?`, `revision_no`, `consequence_type`, `basis`, `quantity numeric(24,8)`, `quantity_unit`, `amount_low/high numeric(24,8)?`, `currency?`, `formula_version`, `overlap_group`, `window_start/end`, `limitations jsonb`, `created_at`; `fc1 / fr1 / fuel_cost / MODELLED / 1.2 L / KES 198â€“238` | FK site/finding revision/assessment/assumption; `UNIQUE(finding_revision_id,consequence_type,revision_no)`; indexes `(site_id,consequence_type,window_start)`, `(finding_revision_id)` | Append-only. Null amount means unpriced; never zero by default. A consequence recomputed with changed assumptions is a new revision. |
| `action` | Durable intervention/work object; `id`; `tenant_id`, `site_id`, `finding_id?`, `asset_id?`, `action_code`, `title`, `owner_id?`, `created_at`; `act1 / s1 / ACT-048 / Review generator policy` | FK site/finding/asset; `UNIQUE(tenant_id,action_code)`; indexes `(site_id,created_at DESC)`, `(finding_id)` | Identity fixed. Current state is reduced from ActionEvents; a Finding may have multiple Actions. |
| `action_event` | Audit trail of acceptance, execution, completion, reopening; `id`; `tenant_id`, `site_id`, `action_id`, `event_type`, `occurred_at`, `recorded_at`, `actor_id`, `payload jsonb`, `idempotency_key`; `ae1 / act1 / WORK_COMPLETED / 12:00 / technician-7` | FK site/action; `UNIQUE(action_id,idempotency_key)`; index `(action_id,recorded_at,id)` | Append-only. `occurred_at` may be backdated; `recorded_at` is server time. Work completion is not proof of benefit. |
| `verification_plan_revision` | Frozen target and guardrails for later evidence; `id`; `tenant_id`, `site_id`, `action_id`, `revision_no`, `baseline_assessment_revision_id`, `target jsonb`, `guardrails jsonb`, `evaluation_window_rule jsonb`, `created_at`, `author_id`; `vp1 / act1 / rev1 / reduce candidate runtime with service preserved` | FK site/action/baseline assessment; `UNIQUE(action_id,revision_no)`; index `(action_id,created_at DESC)` | Append-only; edits make a new plan revision. Cannot evaluate a plan without an accepted baseline. |
| `verification_outcome` | Verdict from later evidence; `id`; `tenant_id`, `site_id`, `plan_revision_id`, `analytic_run_id`, `evaluated_start/end`, `result`, `target_result jsonb`, `guardrail_results jsonb`, `evidence_sha256`, `created_at`; `vo1 / vp1 / ar2 / INCONCLUSIVE / missing service baseline` | FK site/plan/run; `UNIQUE(plan_revision_id,analytic_run_id,evaluated_start,evaluated_end)`; index `(site_id,created_at DESC)` | Append-only. `VERIFIED`, `INEFFECTIVE`, `INCONCLUSIVE`; later records create another outcome revision/run, not an overwrite. |

`FindingRevision` and `FinancialConsequenceRevision` refer to the exact assessment revision they explain. The evidence drawer traverses `finding_revision â†’ assessment_revision â†’ analytic_run_evidence â†’ accepted_evidence â†’ envelope_text`, plus the bound Foundation/SignalBinding and assumption revisions. Deleting a source row is prohibited while a dependent claim exists. Evidence behind a conclusion is not a rendered explanation string.

### Read projections

Start with three transactionally rebuildable tables, not one table per mock screen:

| Table | PK and fields; example | Update rule / indexes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `site_window_projection` | PK `(tenant_id,site_id,domain,window_start,window_end)`; `assessment_revision_ids`, `finding_revision_ids`, `coverage`, `summary jsonb`, `source_watermark`, `model_version`, `built_at`; `s1 / dispatch / [10:00,11:00) / as1 / fi1` | Replace atomically after committed analytic run; indexes `(site_id,window_end DESC)` and `(tenant_id,domain,window_end DESC)`. Rebuild on late evidence/config/model change. |
| `finding_index_projection` | PK `(tenant_id,finding_id)`; `site_id`, `latest_revision_id`, `lifecycle`, `priority_tuple jsonb`, `consequence_ids`, `action_ids`, `updated_at`; `fi1 / fr1 / OPEN` | Upsert from immutable claims/events; indexes `(tenant_id,lifecycle,updated_at DESC)`, `(site_id,lifecycle)`. Priority is a versioned sortable tuple, not a money/health blend. |
| `site_summary_projection` | PK `(tenant_id,site_id)`; `objective_coverage jsonb`, `top_finding_ids`, `consequence_groups jsonb`, `last_received_at`, `analytic_watermark`, `built_at`; `s1 / dispatch=Watch,service=Unknown` | Rebuild from Site windows and work. Index `(tenant_id,built_at)`. Portfolio groups by currency, category, basis and non-overlap; source lag is shown. |

Historical arbitrary windows can query indexed evidence/assessment tables and cache a window projection once requested. Projection rows contain IDs and labels, never substitute for immutable claim/evidence records. A site with no assessment has `Unknown` coverage, not a healthy summary.

## 2. Edge SQLite schema

Use `PRAGMA journal_mode=WAL`, `foreign_keys=ON`, `busy_timeout`, one writer, and a migration table. SQLite is an acquisition ledger, not a product database. Store UTC instants as ISO text. A source stream means `(gateway_id,source_id)`; `sequence_epoch` changes only on explicit reprovisioning, preventing accidental sequence reuse after restore. Local primary keys can be text UUIDs. Example values below correspond to the central examples.

| Table | Key, columns and example | Constraints / write rule |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `gateway_identity` | PK `gateway_id`; `site_id`, `external_id`, `source_mode`, `credential_locator`, `sequence_epoch`, `configured_at`; `g1 / s1 / SIMULATED / epoch-1` | Singleton local row; site/mode fixed after first envelope; no secret in DB. |
| `device_signal_mapping` | PK `(binding_set_id,version,device_id,signal_id)`; `source_id`, `role`, `asset_id`, `source_unit`, `canonical_unit`, `conversion_json`, `valid_from/to`, `snapshot_sha256`; `v3 / generator-controller / status / generator_state` | Immutable registered snapshot with verifiable hash; switch active set atomically. Unknown channels never become envelopes. |
| `sequence_state` | PK `(gateway_id,source_id,sequence_epoch)`; `next_sequence integer`, `updated_at`; `g1 / src1 / epoch-1 / 43` | Increment in same SQLite transaction that inserts outbox row. INTEGER is 64-bit; overflow requires reprovisioned epoch. |
| `envelope_outbox` | PK `message_id`; `gateway_id`, `source_id`, `sequence_epoch`, `sequence`, `source_time`, `envelope_text`, `sha256`, `state`, `created_at`, `last_attempt_at?`; `msg42 / seq42 / PENDING` | `UNIQUE(source_id,sequence_epoch,sequence)`; append bytes once; state `STAGED,READY,IN_FLIGHT,ACKED,DEAD_LETTER`. Real gateways use READY directly; Lab Commit moves sealed STAGED rows to READY. |
| `upload_batch` | PK `batch_id`; `site_id`, `gateway_id`, `source_id`, `foundation_version`, `binding_set_id/version`, `payload_text`, `sha256`, `state`, `attempt_count`, `next_attempt_at`, `created_at`; `batch-007 / hash:â€¦ / READY` | Immutable payload once sealed; `batch_item(batch_id,ordinal,message_id)` with PK `(batch_id,ordinal)` and `UNIQUE(batch_id,message_id)` records order. Retry exact bytes/ID. Split by source and configuration version. |
| `acknowledgement` | PK `(batch_id,ordinal)`; `message_id`, `decision`, `server_evidence_id?`, `server_rejection_id?`, `received_at`, `receipt_sha256`; `batch-007 / 3 / DUPLICATE / e1` | Persist whole verified receipt and disposition transactionally, then mark matching outbox rows ACKED or DEAD_LETTER. No ack from HTTP timeout or an uncommitted/partial server response. |
| `dead_letter` | PK `message_id`; `batch_id`, `kind`, `field`, `detail`, `first_seen_at`, `last_seen_at`, `operator_state`; `msg43 / UNIT_INCOMPATIBLE / OPEN` | Retain rejected/conflicting bytes in outbox; an operator repair creates new mapping/new message if appropriate, never edits this one. |
| `health_counter` | PK `name`; `value integer`, `observed_at`; `mapping_errors / 2` | Local aggregate only. Queue age/depth and disk usage derive from outbox/filesystem; last contact/upload/clock-offset have separate `health_observation(name,value_text,observed_at)` rows. |
| `diagnostic_outbox` | PK `(gateway_id,sequence_epoch,diagnostic_sequence)`; `payload_text`, `sha256`, `state`, `observed_at`, `last_attempt_at?`, `server_received_at?`; `g1 / epoch-1 / 17 / READY` | Allocate sequence and canonical GatewayDiagnosticsV1 bytes in one SQLite transaction. Retry identical bytes/identity. Mark ACKED only after the central row/receipt is durable; a timeout leaves it retryable. Keep STOP pressure visible locally even if publication is blocked. |

**Crash sequence:** device report â†’ map/validate â†’ `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` â†’ read/increment sequence â†’ derive message ID from `(site,gateway,source,epoch,sequence)` â†’ insert canonical envelope and bytes â†’ commit â†’ select READY rows and seal homogeneous batch in a second transaction â†’ POST â†’ verify receipt digest/IDs/count â†’ persist ack and state in one transaction. On restart, reset `IN_FLIGHT` batches to retryable; never recreate a sealed payload. `ACKED` may be compacted after configured retention and backed-up receipt checkpoint; `DEAD_LETTER` and unacknowledged rows remain. Disk pressure stops acquisition visibly rather than dropping evidence silently. If a device provides a durable event ID, store it in the report metadata and use it for duplicate suppression **before** sequence assignment; otherwise repeated physical samples are distinct events and never silently conflated.

## 3. Core data structures and wire contracts

Python `dataclass`/Pydantic and TypeScript interfaces may mirror these shapes. Names below are logical contracts; JSON wire names use `snake_case`. Strict boundary parsing rejects unknown fields, unsupported schema versions, non-finite numbers and ambiguous local timestamps. Canonical JSON has sorted keys, UTF-8, exact decimal strings and a documented hash algorithm. Do not hash a reformatted request body after receipt.

```text
SourceEnvelopeV3 {
  schema: {id:"assetops.source-envelope", version:3},
  message: {message_id:sha256hex, sequence_epoch:string, sequence:int64},
  source: {site_id, gateway_id, source_id, device_id, signal_id},
  mapping: {binding_set_id, version:int},
  timing: {source_time:UTC, published_at:UTC, interval_start?:UTC, interval_end?:UTC},
  payload: {family:MEASUREMENT|POLICY|OPERATIONAL_RECORD,
            value:decimal-string|enum|null, unit:string, measurement?:string,
            quality?:GOOD|SUSPECT|UNAVAILABLE, unavailable_reason?:string,
            record_detail?:versioned-object}
}
IngestionBatchV2 {
  schema: {id:"assetops.ingestion-batch", version:2},
  batch_id, site_id, gateway_id, source_id,
  source_mode:LIVE|SIMULATED,
  configuration: {foundation_version:int, foundation_valid_from:UTC},
  mapping: {binding_set_id, version:int, snapshot_sha256},
  envelopes: [canonical-envelope-text...], payload_sha256
}
BatchReceiptV2 {
  batch_id, payload_sha256, status:IN_PROGRESS|COMPLETED|REFUSED,
  decisions:[{ordinal, message_id?, decision:ACCEPTED|DUPLICATE|REJECTED|CONFLICT,
              evidence_id?, rejection_id?, kind?, detail?}], received_at, receipt_sha256
}
GatewayDiagnosticsV1 {
  schema: {id:"assetops.gateway-diagnostics", version:1},
  site_id, gateway_id, sequence_epoch, diagnostic_sequence,
  observed_at:UTC, last_device_contact_by_source:{source_id:UTC?...},
  queue_count:int, oldest_queue_age_seconds?:int,
  disk_pressure:OK|WARN|STOP, clock_offset_ms?:int,
  mapping_error_count:int, payload_sha256
}
```

The existing v1/v2 envelope and v1 batch parser remain read-compatible for stored demo history. V3 **changes identity semantics**: `message_id = SHA256(length-delimited(site_id,gateway_id,source_id,sequence_epoch,sequence))`, with the tuple durably allocated once. Exact byte framing and test vectors are part of the contract task; do not concatenate without separators or trust a sender-supplied digest. Sequence scope is `(gateway,source,epoch)`; monotonic arrival is not required. `published_at` is when gateway seals publication, not a second measurement time. Source clock uncertainty is expressed as quality/diagnostic metadata, never correction of `source_time`. Batch V2 is homogeneous in source/mapping/Foundation reference; each envelope is still checked against effective configuration at its own time. The batch hash is over canonical bytes excluding the `payload_sha256` field; the receipt hash similarly excludes `receipt_sha256`. Batch `source_mode` is compared with authenticated gateway registration and is only provenance after that check. Wire IDs for site/gateway/source resolve registered UUID rows; device/signal IDs remain scoped source strings. A production batch must not carry Lab-private cause or truth; keep any Lab run reference in a private manifest keyed by batch ID.

`GatewayDiagnosticsV1` is sent independently of observations so an idle or disconnected source can still report gateway state. Its hash uses canonical bytes excluding `payload_sha256`; the registered gateway credential scopes the POST. It never enters `AcceptedEvidence` or analytic input sets. For the S2 demo, Batch V2 uses registered TLS gateway authentication, canonical SHA-256 and exact-byte retries; per-batch cryptographic signing/key rotation are deferred. A later decision to sign must amend this contract and its canonical test vectors before implementation.

```text
SignalBinding {
  binding_revision_id, site_id, gateway_id, source_id, device_id, signal_id,
  asset_id?, role, source_unit, canonical_unit, conversion:{kind,parameters},
  valid_from, valid_to?, recorded_at, binding_set_id, version
}
AcceptedEvidence {
  evidence_id, tenant_id, site_id, message_id, batch_id, source_id, gateway_id,
  sequence_epoch, sequence, source_time, published_at, received_at,
  interval?, family, original:{value,unit}, normalized:{value,unit},
  quality, envelope_sha256, foundation_revision_id, binding_revision_id, source_mode
}
QueryScope {
  tenant_id, site_ids:[id...], window:{start:UTC,end:UTC},
  knowledge_at:UTC, timezone_for_display, selected_revision_id?,
  include_simulated:boolean
}
AnalyticInputSet {
  site_id, analytic_id, model_version, window, knowledge_at,
  evidence_ids:[sorted IDs...], foundation_revision_ids:[...],
  binding_revision_ids:[...], input_sha256, role_coverage:{role:status...}
}
AssessmentRevision {
  assessment_id, assessment_key, revision_no, analytic_run_id, window,
  claim_level, coverage, result, basis_sha256, created_at
}
FindingRevision {
  finding_id, revision_id, revision_no, assessment_revision_id, window,
  title, mechanism, severity, claim_level, limitations, created_at
}
FinancialConsequence {
  consequence_revision_id, finding_revision_id, assessment_revision_id,
  assumption_revision_id?, consequence_type, quantity:{value,unit},
  amount:{low,high,currency}?, basis, formula_version, overlap_group,
  window, limitations
}
```

`QueryScope.window` is half-open `[start,end)` and required for analytic/product queries. `knowledge_at` makes historical audits reproducible; normal UI defaults to current server time. `selected_revision_id` freezes a drill-down even if the surrounding window changes. `AnalyticInputSet.input_sha256` hashes sorted evidence IDs **and their content digests**, resolved configuration/binding revisions, window, analytic/model version and canonical role coverage. The financial consequence has a separate basis digest including its assumption revisions. Missing required roles produce a bounded Unknown/Indeterminate assessment with explicit reason; the analytic never reads world state or a Lab run ID. `FinancialConsequence` has no amount when the technical quantity is supported but price/demand is not. `Measured`, `Modelled`, `Extrapolated` and `Scenario` are distinct bases; UI must expose them.

## 4. Transaction and processing contracts

1. **Register configuration.** Publish Foundation and SignalBinding versions together under Site lock. Validate site type, role vocabulary, units, asset ownership and non-overlapping current valid periods. Persist canonical snapshot hash for authenticated gateway download; no detached snapshot signature or key-rotation system is required in S1â€“S7. Backdated publication enqueues affected windows for recomputation; do not change accepted evidence bytes.
2. **Receive batch.** TLS and gateway credential establish tenant/site/gateway/source. Enforce size/count limits before parsing; resolve external batch ID and digest. Identical retry returns stored receipt; conflicting retry records/refuses without touching prior decisions. A batch with wrong registered mode or configuration reference is refused as a whole.
3. **Decide records.** In one Postgres transaction, lock batch row; for each unprocessed ordinal parse strict envelope, verify identity/sequence and referenced mapping/Foundation validity at `source_time`, normalize with the registered as-accepted conversion, and insert acceptance or rejection plus disposition. Unique message key arbitrates concurrent duplicate deliveries. Equal digest is `DUPLICATE`; changed digest is `CONFLICT` and points to first accepted evidence. Update watermark and create/coalesce recompute jobs in the same commit. Chunk a large batch into short committed transactions; receipt exposes `IN_PROGRESS` until all ordinals are durable. A client retries the same batch/bytes and resumes from the persisted watermark. Return a terminal receipt only after all decisions commit.
4. **Assess.** Worker leases dirty site/domain/window jobs, selects accepted evidence by source time plus interval overlap at a declared knowledge cutoff, resolves the exact public configuration, builds `AnalyticInputSet`, and runs the versioned pure analytic. In one commit insert `AnalyticRun`, immutable assessment/finding/consequence revisions when basis changed, update projections and mark job done. Retry of same input hash is a no-op. Late or out-of-order evidence recomputes the affected and neighboring continuity window; it never mutates old revisions. Model deployment schedules explicit re-evaluation of supported windows.
5. **Verify.** ActionEvent records work. After a later closed window exists, evaluate the frozen plan against accepted evidence, target and each guardrail. Missing coverage is `INCONCLUSIVE`; worsening service prevents `VERIFIED` even if cost falls. Link outcome to exact analytic run/evidence and surface any later superseding outcome.

**Initial API contract:** `POST /api/ingestion/batches` returns terminal `200` receipt or `202 IN_PROGRESS` receipt; `GET /api/ingestion/batches/{id}` returns the same durable state. `409` is reserved for same batch ID/different bytes or registered identity conflict, `422` for malformed contract, `401/403` for authentication/authorization. Normal reads use `GET /api/sites/{id}/evidence`, `/evidence/records/{id}`, `/overview`, `/performance/{view}`, `/findings/{id}` with `from`, `to` and optional `revision_id`; list reads paginate by stable `(source_time,id)` or `(created_at,id)` cursor. All responses include scope/window, revision IDs, evidence coverage and projection watermark. The UI treats a lagging projection as pending, not healthy. Lab Commit calls this same HTTP boundary with a registered simulated gateway; no privileged product-write endpoint exists.

**Gateway diagnostic API:** `POST /api/gateways/{gateway_id}/diagnostics` authenticates the registered gateway/site, checks `GatewayDiagnosticsV1` identity/hash and inserts `gateway_diagnostic` with server `received_at`; an identical retry returns the existing row. `409` refuses identity reuse with different bytes, `422` malformed fields, and `401/403` wrong credential/scope. `GET /api/sites/{id}/gateway-health` returns last server contact, declared cadence, source-contact observations, queue age/depth, disk pressure, clock offset and an explicit `CURRENT|STALE|UNKNOWN` status with diagnostic ID and receive time. It makes no claim that the device or asset itself is healthy. S2-01c publishes locally; S2-02 owns central storage and read API; S7 composes the Site Health claim.

**Demo-to-Postgres cutover:** create Tenant/Site/Gateway/Source registrations from existing Site records, import immutable Foundation and mapping versions, then replay sealed committed batch bytes through the new ingestion service using the legacy parsers. Preserve original batch/message IDs, envelope text, source times and mode; assign new database UUIDs only to internal rows. Compare accepted/rejected/conflict counts and representative evidence/dispatch claims against the file-backed baseline. Keep `var/sites`, Lab runs, stages and committed artifacts untouched and read-only during verification. Switch product reads to Postgres only after the comparison passes; new Lab Commit posts the V2 batch/receipt path. Do not import private world trajectories into the product database. An existing batch whose required public configuration cannot be registered is reported as a migration exception, never guessed into a new Foundation version.

**Acceptance checks for the first slices:** power loss after SQLite enqueue and after server commit; duplicate batch with identical bytes; same ID with different bytes; partial batch crash/retry; reordered and late samples; two same-timestamp samples with different sequences; mapping/Foundation version change during offline period; wrong gateway/site credential; one rejected sibling among valid samples; central diagnostic receipt/reload and stale-heartbeat behavior. S2â€“S7 use simulated input only. At the first real pilot, select one real capture or labelled vendor-format wire fixture and then compare physical and simulated normalized envelopes and downstream claims aside from provenance. Test lineage by opening a visible claim and resolving every evidence and assumption ID, rather than asserting only rendered text.

## 5. Proposed follow-on domain contracts for v6.9 breadth

**Design proposal, not an S1-S7 requirement or owner-approved schema.** The current tables above remain the first vertical contract. The [v6.9 realization map](v69-screen-realization-map.md), [panel contracts](v69-panel-contracts.json), and [model-home inventory](v69-model-homes.json) expose later screens that cannot be built by adding presentation tabs alone. Each family below states its identity, revision, and lineage boundary. A later Architect decision must fix the open domain rule before the Planner activates a packet that depends on it. All proposed rows carry tenant/site scope and same-site foreign keys as in section 1; actor identity and permissions require the auth decision below.

| Family | Proposed entity and identity | Revision/event rule | Evidence and ownership rule | Open domain gate |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Incident case | `incident(id,tenant_id,site_id,incident_key,opened_at,asset_id?,finding_id?)` is a stable operational case, distinct from `action`. An Incident may link multiple Findings, Actions and maintenance requests. | `incident_event(id,incident_id,kind,occurred_at,recorded_at,actor_id,payload,idempotency_key)` is append-only; current status is a projection. Correction adds an event, never erases a case. | Link exact Finding revisions and accepted evidence IDs through scoped junctions. A reported incident without accepted telemetry is still a human report, labelled as such. | Define report/open/resolve/reopen authority, duplicate case matching and whether an outage creates an Incident automatically. |
| Maintenance work | `maintenance_work_request` has stable request identity, Site/Asset and optional Incident/Action links. `maintenance_record` has its own performed-work identity; it is not the request or an `ActionEvent`. `maintenance_plan` is a scheduled intent with Site/Asset scope. | Request state comes from append-only `maintenance_work_event`; a signed/attested record is immutable with correction/supersession. Plans use immutable effective versions; reschedule creates a new version. | Work evidence may include actor-attested records, attachments and accepted post-work measurements. A verification outcome links the exact Action/plan and accepted later window; work completion alone cannot establish restoration. | Define preventive cadence, sign-off, cancellation, recurrence and whether a work request can satisfy several Actions. Do not map request/record/plan into the existing `action` row. |
| Asset registration and warranty | Existing `asset.id` remains physical identity; `asset_registration_revision` records audited name/spec/vendor/serial/location changes with valid and recorded time where claim relevant. `warranty_contract` has separate stable identity, provider and covered asset/period. | Registration changes and retirement are append-only revisions/events. Warranty terms have immutable versions; a claim is a separate event/case, not a boolean on asset. | Stress/readiness claims cite asset revision plus accepted evidence. Warranty eligibility cites exact contract version, event date and supporting work/evidence; a rating or modelled stress alone cannot prove claimability. | Define serial replacement versus same asset, ownership transfer, cover exclusions, vendor identity and monetary valuation basis. |
| Human operational records | `operational_record` has stable record ID, typed kind (such as fuel delivery), author, occurrence interval, recorded time, unit/quantity, source document hash and correction chain. It is separate from `accepted_evidence`, which remains device/gateway sourced. | Attested content is immutable; correction points to superseded record and reason. Reconciliation is a revisioned analytic result over chosen record/evidence IDs. | Fuel balance, service request and backup test calculations cite both operational-record IDs and accepted source evidence IDs with distinct provenance labels. Never manufacture a meter reading from a delivery note. | Define approved record kinds, attestation, unit conversion, fuel reconciliation tolerance and load-request/service-obligation evidence before `FuelBalanceWindow` or `ServiceWindow` claims. |
| Policies and tenant settings | `policy` has stable key and explicit scope (`tenant`, `site`, or domain); `policy_revision` carries valid/recorded time, author and immutable content. Existing `tenant` remains estate identity; `tenant_configuration_revision` owns editable settings once auth exists. | Publishing locks the scope and forbids overlapping current validity per key; corrections append and preserve prior knowledge. Role/permission changes have separate audit events. | Analytic/verification inputs retain exact policy revision IDs. A policy is declared authority, not proof of controller application; applied change has its own artifact/event. | Define policy vocabulary, who may publish, scope precedence, authentication/roles and audit before Settings is writable. |
| Financial scenarios | `financial_scenario` has stable Site/tenant-scoped scenario identity and owner; `financial_scenario_revision` freezes assumption revision IDs, window, parameters and formula version. | A new edit creates a revision; scenario runs/results are append-only, keyed by scenario revision and input digest. | Scenario consequence cites source Finding/quantity revision where used, and is always labelled `Scenario`. It cannot overwrite a measured/modelled operational consequence or be counted as recovered money. | Define allowed variables, cross-site scope, comparison baseline and currency/period rules before the Scenarios tab is live. |
| Site onboarding | `site_draft` has its own UUID and tenant-scoped proposed code, separate from published `site.id`. `site_template` reuses the shipped catalog only as an explicitly versioned input; `site_import_batch` has stable upload identity and per-row disposition. | Draft revisions and setup events are append-only. Publish atomically allocates/resolves the Site code, initial Foundation and source registration; a failed batch row leaves no partial Site. Template updates never rewrite published Sites. | Persist source digest/template version and actor for every published Site; link import-row receipt to resulting Site/Foundation IDs. Imported physical/evidence claims still need gateway registration and accepted envelopes. | Define draft expiration, duplicate-code arbitration, template migration, batch atomicity/per-row retry and tenant authorization. The gated Lab create route is not the product onboarding contract. |

**Shared projection rule.** `CapabilityReadiness`, `FindingIndex`, `SourceHealth`, `AssetHealthSummary` and later `MaintenanceIndex` have multiple screen consumers. Their first implementation must publish a backend read-model contract with scope/window, stable sort/pagination, freshness watermark, revision and drill-down IDs, Unknown/NotApplicable semantics and tenant isolation. A Site projection can feed a global root only after aggregation semantics are declared; shared names in the mock do not imply that the first Site query already serves the estate. `MaintenanceIndex` waits on maintenance identities above.

**Root ownership rule before S4 and S6.** The Site Findings list is a Site-scoped projection; a Finding detail, Analysis, Evidence, Alternatives, Recommendation and History share stable Finding identity in the Findings root. S4's first detail route must preserve that identity and selected revision across tabs and links back to the Site. The Site Work list is a Site-scoped projection; Action Overview, Plan, Execution, Evidence, Verification and History share stable Action identity in the Actions root. Before S6 verification, define the Action-root route and plan/outcome revision URLs, preserving the Site/Finding back-links. Unimplemented tabs stay hidden; an empty tab must represent a real supported question.

**Demo data dependency.** One Kobo window cannot substantiate the multi-site, multi-window, cold-chain or EV mock claims. Before their packets, define a small seed manifest per Site/window with source mode, Foundation/binding versions, gateway fixture, accepted/rejected evidence, analytic version, assumptions and expected claim limits. Rehearse both a complete and an Unknown/contradictory case through normal ingestion; do not stage Findings or prices directly. Portfolio requires comparable periods, currency, coverage and overlap rules across the seeded Sites.
# Site-first execution plan

**Planner handoff Â· 2026-10-06.** The accepted [system architecture](production-shaped-mvp.md) and [platform technical design](production-shaped-mvp-technical-design.md) are frozen inputs. This plan sequences implementation; it does not amend those decisions or revive the old T0xx queue. The seven rows are **product milestones**, each possibly split by the Planner into a few small tasks. A milestone closes only when its named Site mock surface shows real persisted data and the cited evidence can be opened.

## Site product spine

```text
Site Foundation
  â†’ accepted Site Evidence
  â†’ Site Performance / Dispatch
  â†’ Site Finding + Financials
  â†’ Site Overview
  â†’ Site Work / Action / Verification
  â†’ Site Health

simulated or physical report â†’ edge SQLite â†’ SourceEnvelope â†’ shared ingestion
â†’ Postgres accepted evidence â†’ typed analytic input â†’ revisioned claim
â†’ Site read model â†’ screen
```

Start with **one mini-grid Site and one assessed window**. Use the existing causal simulator as the S2â€“S7 producer. A physical protocol capture or labelled vendor-format fixture becomes a contract peer at the first real pilot, once an input is available. Retain the static v6.9 mock screens as interaction/content references; a slice implements the supported portion with honest missing states, not a full static screen filled with invented data. Existing JSON/YAML data stays available for migration comparison, but new product writes use the target stores. The Site shell can keep its current routes and reusable components where they help.

### Seven visible slices

| Slice and mock surface made real | Exact backend/read-model capability unlocked | Reviewable demonstration and completion condition |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **S1 Â· Foundation:** `foundation`, `readiness`, and the configured portion of `sld` | Postgres Tenant/Site/Asset/Gateway/Source registration; immutable valid-time Foundation and SignalBinding revisions; one mini-grid role set and authenticated gateway snapshot download. `GET /api/sites/{id}/foundation?at=` returns the version, topology, bindings and explicit configuration gaps. | Create or import Kobo, inspect device-to-asset/role mappings, change a reserve/control or binding with a new effective version, and reopen an earlier time to see its prior basis. Readiness reports unbound required roles; SLD uses actual registered topology. No live operating status is implied by configured equipment. |
| **S2 Â· Evidence:** `evidence`, `evidence-analysis`, and Lab `gateway-status`/Commit detail | S2-01a reads the registered Foundation/binding basis into Lab runs; S2-01b gives simulated reports a separate SQLite gateway, V3 identity and sealed Batch V2 outbox; S2-01c publishes diagnostics and fault scenarios. S2-02 adds authenticated HTTPS, Postgres accepted/rejected/duplicate decisions, central diagnostics and Evidence queries. The physical protocol adapter is a first-real-pilot gate. | Generate a registered Kobo simulated run, Stage then Commit; disconnect/reconnect the gateway and inspect queue depth, upload result, accepted/delayed/rejected records and central freshness after reload. Replay identical bytes to get duplicates without extra facts. S2 does not claim captured physical-device equivalence. Legacy demo commits remain comparable through migration, not copied as private truth. |

Every packet names **one visible change to a mock surface**, the exact persisted object/read API that makes it real, a runnable demo path, a missing/conflicting-evidence state, and an evidence-lineage check. Tasks may carry migrations, adapters, API and UI together. Do not create isolated â€œPostgres schemaâ€, â€œgateway frameworkâ€, â€œanalytics frameworkâ€ or â€œtab shellâ€ tasks whose only visible result is infrastructure. If a prerequisite needs its own task, its closeout must still change a specific inspectable Site or Lab state and name the next screen it unlocks.

Optimize the critical path for four owner-review moments: **S2 evidence arrives**, **S4 an operational claim has bounded money**, **S6 an intervention has a verified or inconclusive outcome**, and **S7 Site Health completes the mini-grid story**. Use one active slice at a time, small fixtures and focused checks; avoid full estate seed work, extra domains, global indexes and polish until the Site journey works. The target platform contract stays intact, while only robustness required to make the current visible claim credible lands now.


This is a cross-cutting dependency stream alongside the Site screens. It owns source production and delivery, never product claims. The recorded diagnostics contract is a separate authenticated `GatewayDiagnosticsV1` report rather than a `SourceEnvelope`: registered gateway/site identity, observation time, local sequence, last device contact by source, queue count and oldest age, disk pressure, clock offset and mapping errors. The server records its own receipt time and computes central freshness from that time and a declared cadence; a missing heartbeat is Unknown/stale, never a healthy reading. Reports are idempotent by gateway/sequence and are not accepted telemetry. The [technical design](production-shaped-mvp-technical-design.md) now owns the table, endpoint and wire fields; S2-01c/S2-02 packets refine cadence and focused crash tests.

**Physical peer source in effect:** S2 uses simulated reports only. At the first real pilot, choose one real-device capture if available, or one labelled synthetic fixture built from vendor protocol documentation; prove protocol frames, register scaling, units and device clocks before claiming real-adapter equivalence. That future gate replaces the simulator batch while preserving the backend, analytic and UI contracts. It is not an S2 acceptance check. The S7 demo cannot use captured-device replay as a battery fallback under this source choice.

## Shared implementation rules

- **Immediate demo scope versus target design:** S1â€“S2 need crash-safe SQLite enqueue, stable identities, authenticated batch ingestion, durable per-record decisions and Postgres Site/evidence storage. Build the minimal diagnostics needed to inspect delivery. Detailed queue retention policy, elaborate backpressure controls, multi-protocol drivers, broker transport, full bitemporal editing UI and generic streaming infrastructure can follow when pilots require them. Database/wire schemas should leave room without falsely claiming those behaviors are shipped.
- **Typed analytic boundary:** `EvidenceQuery` returns typed normalized readings/records with quality, time, asset/role, configuration basis and accepted-evidence IDs. The dispatch analytic consumes this plus a typed public configuration view. SQL rows, `jsonb`, canonical envelope text, Lab world records and source adapters stop at repository/normalization boundaries. Contract tests compare simulated and captured real inputs after this boundary.
- **Episode identity:** decide continuity tolerance, episode anchor and recurrence linkage in S3/S4 domain design, with a short decision table before coding the first Finding. `Finding.id` names a durable operational episode; `FindingRevision` describes reassessment of that episode. A new separated episode is a new Finding, linked as recurrence if relevant. Do not use a changing model output hash as Finding identity.
- **Domain algorithms:** dispatch thresholds, cold-chain excursion detection and EV session logic are domain-design work. The platform plan requires their inputs, revision/lineage outputs and claim limits, not more universal framework or algorithm prose. S1â€“S7 cover one mini-grid Site; later domains add role sets and analytics through the same seam.
- **Verification gates:** each slice exercises the screen at its selected window, the read API, storage/reload, a missing/contradictory evidence case, and lineage back to an accepted SourceEnvelope. S2 adds loss/retry/duplicate/conflict; S3â€“S4 add late evidence and revision preservation; S6 adds guardrail failure. Tests should assert behavior, not mock labels.

## Handoff beyond Site

After S4, a **Findings index** can already list real Finding objects, but build the global root only when the Site detail and lifecycle are useful. After S6, **Actions** and **Sites** become filtering/indexing over durable Site/Action summaries; **Portfolio** aggregates comparable consequences and priorities with currency/window/coverage boundaries; **Evidence** aggregates Site capability/readiness; **Assets** indexes Site Asset/Health/work records after S7. These are later product roots, not prerequisites or empty screens for the Site spine. Cold-chain and EV are later Site-domain passes using the same ingestion, revision, work and shell contracts; neither should be built by copying mini-grid screens with different labels.

**Planner output requested next:** perform the archive and routing update, publish the new S1â€“S7 queue, and prepare S1 packets with mock-screen state, API/read-model contract, migration seam, acceptance fixture, reviewer gate and explicit deferments. Do not activate or implement an old T0xx task merely because its title resembles a new result.

.ai/DECISIONS.md:3944:Persist at most one immutable diagnostic per declared real-UTC cadence slot; a repeat returns its exact stored identity and bytes, and a clock earlier than the latest slot refuses. State changes within a slot are visible in the local read immediately but reach the wire in the next slot; the server uses receive time and cadence to decide central freshness. `queue_count` is the number of `STAGED|READY|IN_FLIGHT` envelopes, sealed or unbatched, excluding holds and dead letters; queue age uses their real-UTC outbox creation time and must remain real UTC when Lab V3 `published_at` becomes virtual. Contact is gateway real-UTC observation of a public report that resolves to a permitted source, even on replay or later hold; never-heard remains null. For a SIMULATED gateway its provenance is `OBSERVED_SIMULATED_REPORT`, never proof of physical controller contact or source health.
